# VOLUME 08, BATCH 0004 — the authoritative block for the current phase, and it wins over every block above it

Current phase: **volume-08-batch-0004 (COMPLETE. TEN CHAPTERS ON DISK: Chapters 0381 to 0390, days 381 to 390, one chapter to one day, from a Thursday to a Saturday, the Bare Month days sixty-six to seventy-five, the ordinal being the day less 315. `bible/power-system.md` §49 EXISTS, `state/batch-summaries/volume-08-batch-0004.md` is written, and ten entries stand at the foot of `state/chapter-summaries.md`. NO DECISION MOVED: no day, no weekday, no month day, no anchor, no live thread's state, no card, no outline, and none of the eight decisions of record was altered, and §§32, 33, 33A and 33B were not amended. THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-08/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`, AND IT IS A BATCH AND NOT THE CLOSE.)**

**THIS RUN RESUMED FROM A CHECKPOINT AND THE TEN CHAPTER FILES ALREADY EXISTED, so this phase's work on them was a repair pass and not a first draft. That is recorded here, in the batch record and in the chapter summaries, because a batch that resumes mid-run and then publishes one set of figures is the third version of the failure this repository names.**

**THE RUN WAS BUILT FROM DAY 1 BEING A TUESDAY AND FROM NOTHING ELSE before any prose was written, and then checked against the batch prompt's table row by row, and all ten rows agree: 381 Thursday, 382 Friday, 383 Saturday, 384 Sunday, 385 Monday, 386 Tuesday, 387 Wednesday, 388 Thursday, 389 Friday, 390 Saturday. There is no double day in this batch and there is no double day in this volume.**

**THE TEN DAYS IN ONE LINE. A man who reads a page in a room was told out loud, in front of about nine people, by the woman who writes this city's notices, that she was not going to ask him what is in the inside of his coat and that the not-asking has cost her the same as asking would have, and nobody in that room thanked her for it and she had not asked. Two men who have never asked each other anything in a room divided nine steps between them without anybody asking either of them to. Three barrows went up wet market boards, two of which have belonged to nobody since before the water, and a barrow that will not go up wet has to go up on its shaft and the shaft takes the load into whoever is holding it, and a man's right hand has not closed since. A man who has put a number in a column four times a year for nine years said the number out loud on a wharf in his own mouth and said that there is a second one and would not say which of the two he believes. A woman read the four places under a heading out loud in the local language in a room with the day on the door, and the fourth one is a household's, and the household is not going to be asked, and the room knows that the cost of an empty column is a woman on a step and not a column. Thirty-nine men under a gate were told out loud on a road that they are three hundred days unpaid, measured from the eighty-fifth day of this flood, and nobody argued with the number, and a ruled line at the bottom of a column is still ruled and still empty. A sheet of road was put on a step in the open in front of one woman and refused, and the woman who put it there said out loud that the refusal was of her own decision and not of her offer. A man with nine words in his coat and a man with a piece of chalk in his breast pocket stood nine feet apart in front of a wall with a shape going off it and neither of them wrote anything on it. A room ruled the seventh sheet and did not carry it down the stair. The seventh and last sheet went out at the sixth hour to a place and not to a house, four days, unsigned, with nothing at the top of it, and the man who carries it has never opened one, and the man of about thirty-nine said out loud on a wharf that he is not going to be sent for the room. A room spent a Saturday doing nothing and agreed out loud that what three people take out of it will not be written down. Nothing was measured, dug, counted, probed or resolved, and no character in any of the ten goes and looks at the line in the ground.**

**AND TWO FIGURES THE FIRST PASS OF THESE TEN FILES PRINTED WERE WRONG, AND NEITHER WAS THE TRAP FIGURE.** **Chapter 0385 said *this morning is the two hundred and eighty-fifth day of this flood* in a chapter that is day 385; it now reads the three hundred and eighty-fifth, in the same sentence, with both anchors named. Chapter 0390 dated the room's not-asking to *a Thursday a week ago* and *held for eight days*, sixteen days after that Thursday; both now read sixteen days, with the event named in the sentence.** Eight further reference faults are listed in `state/batch-summaries/volume-08-batch-0004.md` §9 item 9 and were all repaired, and **the trap figure itself was correct and was checked three times: 385 − 85 = 300, and the sentence that prints it names the first of March, the eighty-fifth, the three hundred and eighty-fifth and the anchor twice.**

**THE THINGS THAT ARE NOW ON A PAGE, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM IS A GAIN AND NONE OF THEM IS A POWER.** A price for not asking, given out loud by the person who pays it. A man with a hazel mallet who came up a wet hill to be seen carrying it. A hand that took a barrow on the right and could not close again, and a man who knew and did not say that he knew. Two numbers and a refusal to say which one is true. A share read out loud and found to be a household's, and an empty column that goes out correct. A figure said aloud on a road that cannot be got back off anybody. A page offered in the open and refused, and the reason the refusal was of a decision and not of an offer. A man with a chalk in his pocket saying out loud that he has nothing of his own to put on a wall. A seventh sheet made and not sent. A seventh sheet sent, unsigned, to a place, and a carrier who has never opened one. A blank where a column would go. **None of them is a power. Some of them are the price of a power. All of them are heavier than a power.**

**AND THE HOUSE FIGURES, MEASURED AND NOT WRITTEN, WITH THE DEFINITIONS BESIDE THEM, AND THE FIRST EDITIONS BESIDE THE PUBLISHED ONES.** The share of words inside bold marks, heading line included, `---` rules counted, bold marks counted as nothing, file split on whitespace: **8.23, 9.27, 19.04, 15.67, 9.33, 22.94, 8.67, 15.98, 12.62 and 8.54 per cent, a range of 8.23 to 22.94, a mean of 13.03 unweighted and 13.0 weighted by words on 15,602 words, against a first edition of 8.25, 9.15, 17.90, 16.16, 9.02, 21.91, 7.83, 15.26, 13.33 and 8.05 on 15,851 words, and no target and no verdict is set on any of them.** The longest bolded turn in the ten is **eighty-eight words**, against a first edition of one hundred and thirty-five; five turns were over the working ceiling of about ninety and four over the hard one of ninety-five, and all five were repaired with a plain-speech interjection and a narration beat and none with a cut sentence. The longest run of consecutive speech paragraphs is **three**, against a ceiling of four, and five runs were at four on the first pass. Long sentences of forty words or more: **177, none duplicated**, checked against all thirty files of the volume. The count of paragraphs carrying neither speaker marker: **zero in all ten.** The exchange, thirty-two plain speeches and fifteen answered, forty-six point nine per cent, against a first edition of fourteen of twenty-six. **The chorus frame on the bare head *about four people in* is five in two hundred and twenty-one paragraphs, one in 44.2, and on the variant-agnostic instrument *about four people* plus any word it is ten in the same paragraphs, one in 22.1, against a first edition of twenty-two and forty-six in two hundred and two — and the question the count does not answer is answered in the batch record: of the forty-six, thirty-eight restated a sentence the paragraph had already made and eight carried something new.**

**ADRIAN IS NAMED IN ONE OF THE TEN AND IN THE OTHER NINE HE IS IN NO CHAPTER IN ANY FORM, and the one is 0381, and the plan's own §4 list is what that follows.** In 0381 he is in a room reading a page that is not his, with a sheet folded into four in the inside of his coat with a date on the back of it and two names on the front of it and the coat buttoned over it, and nobody in that room asked him one question and the woman who keeps the register said out loud in front of about nine people that she was not going to ask and gave the price of the not-asking. **He is Stage 2 on all ten days, no working was performed, no threshold was opened, the other world is not named once, he is aged nowhere, he was offered no workway and asked for none, *about two hundred rooms* is at zero across the ten files and is forbidden in his chapter, and the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the ten files.**

**AND THE FOUR DEBTS CARRY FORWARD WHOLE AND UNPAID AND THIS BATCH REPAIRED NONE OF THEM AND TOOK NONE OF THEM**, and the fifth debt — *about two hundred rooms* on three men, two of whom share a nine in a second place — is carried too and **stands at zero in all ten of these chapters, and the paragraph in Chapter 0390 that wanted an observer who has been in a great many rooms uses the second handle instead of the figure.** The six debts from the Batch 0003 review are carried in `state/open-threads.md` and are not resolved here.

**THE VOLUME'S OWN NAME IS STILL A MAN'S ANSWER SPOKEN ONCE IN CHAPTER 0357 AND WRITTEN DOWN IN NO FILE, and this block does not record it and neither does the batch record nor any of the ten chapters.**

**THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-08/batch-0005/PROMPT.md` AND IT IS A BATCH, NOT THE CLOSE.** It writes Chapters 0391 to 0400, days 391 to 400, from a Sunday to a Tuesday, and it is told that day 391 is when three people go down the road with a barrow each, day 393 is the climax and the volume's one panel, day 395 is the register line with a day on it and nothing under the day, day 396 is the new question asked and not answered, and day 400 is the fence and the road going away under it, and that four of the seven round-figure traps fall on day 400.

# VOLUME 08, BATCH 0004 — REVIEW REPAIR PASS — the authoritative block, and it wins over every block above it

**A review of this batch raised eight findings. Six were in the prose, one was a figure this batch's own record published wrong, and one was in the next phase's prompt. All six prose faults, the figure and the three prompt faults are repaired. No chapter was restarted, no plot, no beat, no day, no weekday, no month day, no anchor, no cast member and no decision of record moved, and no controller file was touched.** The disposition is `reviews/volume-08/batch-0004.md`; the batch record carries the pages at `state/batch-summaries/volume-08-batch-0004.md` §11 and the figures that moved at its §9.11 and §9.12.

**WHAT WAS REPAIRED IN THE PROSE, IN SIX PLACES AND FIVE OF THEM ARE ONE OR TWO SENTENCES.** **A man was in two places at once in Chapter 0381 and was carrying another man's room count while he was at it:** line 13 put the man of about thirty-nine against the wall by the step at the back where the light does not reach, and line 55 put him in the band of light at the near end of the bench, and he is against the wall at the end of the bench nearest the step now, which is inside the light and true of the two men line 55 names. The gloss on line 23 gave the man of about thirty-four a count of seven rooms at the back in a fortnight, and that counter belongs to the man of about thirty-nine and runs to eight in the same chapter at line 43, so it is gone and the thirty-four has his own two weeks from line 13 instead, at the near end of that bench, with no figure on him. **The hours ran backwards, in two chapters the review named and one it did not:** Chapter 0383's man of about thirty-nine arrived at the ninth hour and left at the fourth, and he goes up the path at about the second hour of the afternoon now, in the form this volume already uses at Chapter 0390 and Chapter 0361, because the clock runs the morning and the afternoon as two passes; Chapter 0387's gate was barred at the seventh hour while two of the three people on that lane had not arrived yet, and the bar goes across at the tenth now, which is the hour after the woman of about fifty comes out and says a date to a post; and Chapter 0382's man of about thirty-nine came along the boards at the ninth hour and went up them at the sixth, in a day that runs two, three, four, five, six and a light at the seventh, and he comes along at about the third now, which is the hour the first barrow goes up. **The third is the review's own rule applied and not a new finding: once *a chapter that advances an hour has to be read against the hour its own lead establishes* is a rule rather than a reading, the sentence has to be checked in every chapter that carries it.** **Chapter 0382's governing exchange named nobody** — *You are not going to say whose those two are* / *I am not going to say whose those two are* / *The wheels are dry* / *The wheels are dry and they have been since before the water* — in the chapter that carries thirty of the batch's thirty-one barrow mentions, and two narration beats went in naming the man of about thirty-eight as the one who came to ask and the man of about thirty-four as the one being asked, and no speech was touched and the bold-and-plain alternation is unchanged. **Two tenses in Chapter 0382 had been swapped by the checkpoint repair and one prophecy announced the section immediately below it**, and the clause under *had worked out since that* is past now and the prophecy is cut, and the declared device in the rest of those lines was not cut. **And Chapter 0387 read *a wall over a board of boards*, which is the only occurrence of that phrase in forty files, and it reads *a wall over a board*, which is the stallman's second handle and the thing he says out loud seven lines later.**

**AND THE SEVENTH FAULT WAS IN THIS BATCH'S OWN RECORD AND IN THE BIBLE, and it is the worst place a fault of this kind can sit.** The declared string *about four people in* was published at **zero in six of the ten**, with a list of five chapters and a truncated fragment standing in for the sixth. It is at zero in **five** of the ten — 0381, 0382, 0383, 0385 and 0386 — and Chapter 0390's count is one. **The counts, the per-chapter series, the totals and both denominators were right, and the sentence that interpreted them was wrong, and a wrong interpretation inside the one item whose job is to certify that the batch watched itself is worse than a wrong count, because a count can be re-run and an interpretation has to be believed.** Both files are corrected in place and the first edition is published beside it. **It is a fat-finger in a summary clause, and it is the first of this fault's three versions that has not been a date, and the three dates are the reason a shorthand needs an anchor.**

**AND THE NEXT PHASE'S PROMPT WAS REPAIRED IN THREE PLACES, and it is a file the next writer opens before it writes a word, so a fault left in it is a fault handed forward on purpose.** **Its object table sent both the second slate and the folded sheet of road to Chapter 0392**, which is the only one of its ten chapters that has Adrian Vale in it, and the sheet lives in Tamsin Quill's apron, so a 0392 that named it puts both women of twenty-nine in one room in the one chapter that carries him and prohibition 13 forbids exactly that: the table now sends the register and the slates to 0392 and the folded sheet of road to 0399, and prohibition 13 carries the reason in full. **Its object table gave the boards and the bare wall to Chapter 0400 alone**, where `outline/volume-08.md` §20.10 gives it to 0397 and 0400 and the prompt's own item 5 had already given day 397 the fence and the road; the table now reads 0397 and 0400. **And it stated that the man of about thirty-nine has no room count of his own on any page, which is false on seven of this volume's own ten days**, and it now carries the run: four rooms on days 372 and 375, a sixth time on 379, six on 380, eight on 381, nine on 384, ten on 388 and eleven on 390. **No day, no chapter number, no object count that holds, no prohibition and no decision of record in that prompt moved.**

**THE FIGURES THE SIX REPAIRS MOVED, AND THE ONES THEY DID NOT.** The bolded share of Chapter 0382 went **9.27 to 8.73** and Chapter 0383's **19.04 to 19.01**, the unweighted mean **13.03 to 12.97**, the weighted mean **13.0 to 12.92**, the words **15,602 to 15,710**, the paragraphs **221 to 223**, the chorus frame's two denominators **44.2 to 44.6** and **22.1 to 22.3**, the long sentences **177 to 179** with Chapter 0382 at **19 to 21**, the longest run inside the ten **38 to 37** and now between Chapters 0384 and 0390, the run between 0381 and 0390 **38 to 34** and now a different frame, the run between 0381 and 0384 **36 to 24**, and two object occurrences — the barrows in Chapter 0382 **28 to 30** and the boards in Chapter 0387 **26 to 25**. **Six of the twelve moved because the repairs added about a hundred and seven words of attribution and narration, and none of them moved because a chapter got shorter.** Unchanged and checked again on the files: the range of the bolded share, the longest bolded turn at eighty-eight words, the count of paragraphs carrying neither speaker marker at zero in all ten, the exchange at fifteen of thirty-two and forty-six point nine per cent with the same per-chapter series, the longest run of consecutive speech paragraphs at three, the chorus frame at ten on (B) and five on (A) with a maximum of one per `---` section in all ten, the counting device once in 0381 and once in 0390 and its family once in 0382, 0383, 0388 and 0389, the forty-three weekday tokens at 3, 4, 6, 4, 4, 1, 4, 3, 3 and 11, and *passage*, *privilege*, *holder* and *volume* at zero across the ten files.

**AND WHAT DID NOT MOVE, WHICH IS THE HALF OF A REPAIR PASS THAT MATTERS MOST.** The seven notices still stand at 351→355, 354→358, 359→363, 362→366, 366→370, 371→375 and 389→393, and 389 is the seventh and the last. The three hundred days unpaid under the near gate is still measured from the first of March, which is the eighty-fifth day of this flood, on the three hundred and eighty-fifth, and no other chapter of the ten prints it. The bare month day still prints as the day less 315 and the eighty-fifth is still the last day of it any chapter of this volume may print. Adrian Vale is in Chapter 0381 and in no other of the ten, in any form, and he still reads a page that is not his and is asked nothing and performs no working. The wall said nothing on all ten days and the volume's one panel is still fixed to day 393. The day-154 sentence is still not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on, and the man who is right about it is still the man of about thirty-nine in Chapter 0393. The count of this city's silences is 290 at day 390. Nobody counted the days since the fourth of the four said no. **The volume's own name is still a man's answer spoken once in Chapter 0357 and written down in no file, and this block does not have it either.**

**THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-08/batch-0005/PROMPT.md` AND IT IS A BATCH, NOT THE CLOSE, and it is the last batch of Volume 08.** It writes Chapters 0391 to 0400, days 391 to 400, from a Sunday to a Tuesday, and it is told that day 391 is when three people go down the road with a barrow each, day 393 is the climax and the volume's one panel, day 395 is the register line with a day on it and nothing under the day, day 396 is the new question asked and not answered, and day 400 is the fence and the road going away under it, and that four of the seven round-figure traps fall on day 400, and that **the register and the slates are in 0392 and the folded sheet of road is in 0399 and not in 0392, and the boards and the bare wall are in 0397 and 0400, and the man of about thirty-nine has a room count and it runs.**

# VOLUME 08, BATCH 0005 — the authoritative block for the current phase, and it wins over every block above it

Current phase: **volume-08-batch-0005 (COMPLETE, AND IT IS THE LAST BATCH OF VOLUME 08. **THIS RUN WAS NOT A RESUMPTION. The ten chapter files did not exist when it began and there was no checkpoint, and every repair recorded below was made inside the ten files before this block was written, and both editions of every figure that moved are at `state/batch-summaries/volume-08-batch-0005.md` §9.**

**THE RUN WAS BUILT FROM DAY 1 BEING A TUESDAY AND FROM NOTHING ELSE before any prose was written, and then checked against the batch prompt's table row by row, and all ten rows agree: 391 Sunday, 392 Monday, 393 Tuesday, 394 Wednesday, 395 Thursday, 396 Friday, 397 Saturday, 398 Sunday, 399 Monday, 400 Tuesday. There is no double day in this batch and there is no double day in this volume.** The run was then validated by recomputing days 301 to 350 out of the same two founding figures and reproducing `bible/power-system.md` §33A item 4's table, at zero mismatches.

**THE TEN DAYS IN ONE LINE, and it is the resolution, the new question and the last image, and the volume is closed on the page and not on a page about a page.** Three people went down a road out of this city on a Sunday morning with a barrow each and nothing to fetch, and nobody is looking for anything, and a name was said out loud in daylight in a room with a date on the door and was written down nowhere including in this file, and a page with that name at the top of it went out of this city on the seventy-eighth day of that month with four places under a heading and one of them a household's and one of them empty on purpose, and a man was right about the column and was not thanked and was not argued with, and the wall said one thing and it was refused in six words in about four seconds and the page went anyway. The man of about thirty-eight, who filled in the rest of that page correctly out of what he is given, said out loud on a wharf on the seventy-ninth day that the price of a name at the top of a page is not paid by the woman whose name it is and not by the woman who wrote it and is paid by him, and nobody thanked him. A register carries a day with nothing under it. A woman of about sixty-nine asked who a fence is for, to a room and not to anybody, and nobody answered it, and neither of the two men it is about was asked. A page with about nine places on it came out of an apron on a wharf and went back into it. A market roof went back up. A man put his hand on a wall with the shape of nine boards gone off it as he went past. And on the eighty-fifth day of the Bare Month a man of about fifty-nine came up a shut road, put his barrow down on the low side of a fence of sixteen posts and eleven withies, saw three people on the road below him, and did not go after them.

**AND THE THINGS THAT ARE NOW ON A PAGE, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM IS A GAIN AND NONE OF THEM IS A POWER.** A name said out loud and not written down, and a page with a name at the top of it going out four days later. A man right about a column in one turn of speech with nobody arguing and nobody thanking him. A refusal in six words of a rule that told a room he did not have to have answered a question nine years ago. A man saying out loud on a wharf in a wind what a column costs and who pays it. A day entered in a register with nothing under the day and the third column empty on purpose. A question about a fence asked to a room and answered by nobody, asked by the same woman who asked the last one and got nothing. A page taken out of an apron and given to nobody. A roof back up. A hand on a wall on the way past. **None of them is a power. Some of them are the price of a power. All of them are heavier than a power.**

**AND ONE FIGURE THE BATCH PROMPT GAVE THAT WAS WRONG, AND IT IS CORRECTED HERE AND THE CORRECTION IS ON A PAGE.** The prompt said the man of about thirty-nine is at about nine rooms on day 393. **The run on the files is four at the back of a room on days 372 and 375, a sixth time on 379, six on 380, eight on 381, nine on 384, ten on 388 and eleven on 390, and `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0390.md:35` prints eleven in a fortnight three days before day 393, and a figure that goes down from eleven to nine over three days is a subtraction taken across the wrong boundary. NO ROOM COUNT IS PRINTED FOR HIM IN ANY OF THE TEN CHAPTERS and the three of them in which he is in a room say instead that he had been at the back of every room this city has had for about a fortnight and had been asked one question in none of them. The prompt's line is withdrawn and this block is the correction.**

Long sentences of forty words or more: **170, none duplicated**, checked against all fifty files of the volume, per chapter 18, 25, 21, 15, 16, 17, 14, 13, 15 and 16. The count of paragraphs carrying neither speaker marker: **zero in all ten.** The exchange, **twenty-one plain speeches and sixteen answered, seventy-six point two per cent.** The chorus frame on the bare head *about four people in* is **four in two hundred and thirty-three paragraphs, one in 58.2**, and on the variant-agnostic instrument *about four people* plus any word it is **fourteen in the same paragraphs, one in 16.6**, **against a first edition of eighteen and fifty-four in two hundred and thirty-seven, one in 4.4, which is exactly Batch 0004's first-pass figure and is the finding this block leads with.** **Forty of the fifty-four were cut and re-attached to the sentence they were standing in and fourteen were kept, and the fourteen are named one by one at `state/batch-summaries/volume-08-batch-0005.md` §3 item 4c, and the maximum is one per `---` section in all ten files on both instruments.** **AND THE THIRD EDITION'S THREE FIGURES THAT WERE NOT FIGURES AT ALL: the batch record's first edition said thirteen in two hundred and thirty-five and one in 18.1 for the same measurement this block had at fourteen, and its second edition said a hundred and seventy-one long sentences in one place and a hundred and seventy in another with a per-chapter list that added up to a hundred and seventy-one, and all three were the same figure counted three ways on two files, and the record now stands at fourteen in two hundred and thirty-three, one in 16.6, and a hundred and seventy long sentences.**

**ADRIAN IS NAMED IN ONE OF THE TEN AND IN THE OTHER NINE HE IS IN NO CHAPTER IN ANY FORM, and the one is 0392, and the plan's own §4 list is what that follows.** In 0392 he is in a room reading the register, which is not his, with the third column of it empty and his finger about a finger's width back from the edge of it, and nobody in that room asked him one thing. **He is Stage 2 on all ten days, no working was performed, no threshold was opened, the other world is not named once, he is aged nowhere, he was offered no workway and asked for none, *about two hundred rooms* is at zero across the ten files and is forbidden in his chapter, and the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the ten files. ## VOLUME 08 CLOSE — REVIEW REPAIR PASS, and this block wins over everything above it

**A review ran on the Volume 08 close phase and returned ten findings. Eight are repaired, one is declined with a reason that is the runner's, one is confirmed with nothing to do, and three further faults were found by this pass while verifying the review's own arithmetic. The record is `reviews/volume-08/close.md`. No chapter was written, restarted or repaired; nothing in Volume 08 moved; `outline/volume-08.md` and `outline/ending.md` were not opened; the ending did not move; no new final enemy was introduced.**

**THE ONE THAT MATTED MOST IS A PROSE FIX, NOT A FIGURE, AND IT IS THE HOLD.** All six bolded spans of Chapter 0357 were extracted programmatically without printing any of them and searched across the whole repository: each is in exactly one file, the chapter it is on the page of. **What was found is a different speech in the same room — a ten-word verbatim run of the woman of about twenty-nine's line reproduced in `state/volume-08-close.md` and `state/character-state.md`, one line away from a description of a different event four days later that reads almost the same.** It is paraphrased now in both. **The rule that comes out of it: the hold covers a held sentence, and a state file may not copy a mouth at all, because a page in this book costs something to produce and a file in this repository is not a page.**

**THE FIGURES. Four vocabulary counts one instance high (*man* 6,613→6,612, *thing* 5,177→5,176, *person* 3,247→3,246, *road* 1,988→1,987; the other nine exact). Four crowded-descriptor figures that do not reproduce on the denominator they were published against, and the reason is not arithmetic: the close carried them out of `outline/volume-08.md` §20.8, which correctly labels them the crowded four of the 350-file map, and printed them as 400-file figures, and all four reproduce exactly on 350 files (*woman of about thirty-four* 351/142→362/147, *man of about thirty-four* 283/108→341/139, *man of about thirty-nine* 133/69→177/93, and *man of about forty-four* 162/85 on both — one figure in four reproducing by accident is what let the swap survive). **`outline/volume-08.md` §20.8 is not at fault and is not amended; its figures are right and are about 350 files.** And a pool described with the count of the crowd in it — the close said *nineteen descriptors are at zero* and listed twelve; nineteen is the count standing on Volume 08's files and twelve is the pool, and it is corrected to twelve.** Every first edition is withdrawn in the file it was wrong in and not deleted. **The descriptor decisions are not re-decided: the pool is still the pool, the crowded four are still barred from a new person, and the corrected numbers make that bar stronger.**

**THE BIBLE. Four factual errors in `bible/power-system.md` §51, none of which moved a day: day 440 called a Saturday where it is a Sunday and the section's own table says otherwise; a heading that said fifteen round figures over eighteen rows; a stated reason for day 450's weekday that did not derive the result; and day 425 called the midpoint of a fifty-day range that has two middle days.** §51 still amends nothing in §§32 to 33B, and its fifty-row day table and twenty-one interval anchors recompute at zero mismatches.

**AND A STALE POINTER THAT FAILED INVISIBLY.** A published fault pointed at `state/character-state.md:2260`; that file is 935 lines long and the fault is at line 223, and it is the same line as the odd bold-mark count, so the two published faults are one line and one pair of characters. The pointer is corrected and the two characters are routed unrepaired, because guessing at one of nine bold marks in a state file a writer reads first makes a worse file than the fault.

**THE TWO BLOCKING FINDINGS. `workspace/volume-09/outline/PROMPT.md` was named as the successor in six files and was never written; it is written now, and its existence is what stops the runner seeding a generic continuation prompt that would plan Volume 09 and write Chapters 0401 onward in the same run. `workspace/volume-08/close/.done` is absent and is NOT hand-written, because `scripts/novel_runner.sh` writes it after this fix invocation returns and a marker that already exists makes the completion commit find nothing to change and exit before the WIP branch is cleared — the absence is the normal state of a phase whose runner has not finished, exactly as `state/current.md:90` recorded on an earlier phase of this volume.**

**THE SEVEN DEBTS ARE ALL UNPAID AND ALL RE-MEASURED, PLUS AN EIGHTH FOUND HERE, AND THIS PASS TOOK NONE OF THEM.** All eight are in the successor prompt by name and at their length. **The four decisions of record stand: the voice is confirmed, the line is still an audited candidate and not an adoption, the calendar is still §51's and the Bare Month still does not end, and the cast is still the measured pool — two of the figures printed beside them were wrong and are corrected, and a correction is not a re-decision.**

**ADRIAN VALE IS IN NINE OF THE FIFTY CHAPTERS — 0402, 0405, 0414, 0422, 0428, 0432, 0436, 0441, 0447 — and in no chapter in any form in the other forty-one, and in eight of the nine he is in a room reading a page. He is Stage 2 on all fifty days, performs no working, opens no threshold, is offered no workway, and the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the volume.**

**THE FIGURE THIS PHASE FOUND THAT THE STATE LAYER GETS WRONG, AND IT IS A FALSE ZERO ABOUT A HELD STRING. The six-word refusal spoken by the man of about thirty-eight in Chapter 0393 is on a page once and is ALSO reproduced verbatim inside bold marks at `bible/power-system.md:1686`, which is §50, Volume 08 Batch 0005's permanent witness section. `state/volume-08-close.md` §4 and `reviews/volume-08/close.md` §6 both say it is nowhere else in this repository, and both are false: the close's review extracted the spans from Chapter 0357 and searched, and the six words live in a different chapter, and a hold measured from one chapter does not cover the other. The words are not printed in this block and were not printed in any file this phase wrote, and this phase did not repair it, because §50 is a batch's witness and a phase that quietly rewrites a witness to make a state file true is doing the thing this repository exists to catch. It is routed to a Volume 08 review repair pass. The seven-word answer in Chapter 0357 is clean.**

**AND THE INSTRUMENT FINDING, WHICH IS AGAINST AN INHERITED CORRECTION. The published definition of the thirteen-row vocabulary row — a token bounded by whitespace, punctuation or a line end — does not say what a hyphen and an apostrophe are, so five readings were run and all five are published at `outline/volume-09.md` §19.1. The reading that reproduces the most of the row is the one where a letter, a hyphen and an apostrophe all continue a word: nine of thirteen exact. The four that differ are each exactly one high and each is the figure the close's own first edition printed, and the four the close's review repair pass subtracted one from reproduce on no reading this phase could construct. Both editions stand and the table is at §19.2. The tell is the shape: the nine that agree are the nine where the unstated flag cannot matter and the four that differ are the four where it can.**

**THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-09/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, AND IT IS A BATCH AND NOT AN OUTLINE. It writes Chapters 0401 to 0410, days 401 to 410, from a Wednesday to a Friday, ten chapters, finished prose, and it decides nothing.**

# VOLUME 09 OUTLINE — REVIEW REPAIR PASS — the authoritative block, and it wins over every block above it

**A REVIEW OF THE VOLUME 09 OUTLINE PHASE RAN AND ITS FINDINGS ARE IN `logs/outline.review.log`, and this block is the record of what was repaired, what was struck, what was withdrawn, and what was left alone. Eight blocking defects and one wording risk were raised. All eight and the risk are closed. NO CHAPTER WAS WRITTEN IN THIS PASS, NO PLOT WAS CHANGED, NO CARD WAS REMOVED, AND NO PROSE WAS TOUCHED.**

**AND THE PATTERN THE EIGHT SHARE IS PUBLISHED FIRST, because a repair pass that does not name the class of fault it is repairing teaches the next reader nothing. Four of the eight were a figure lifted from the wrong place and published in a file a later writer inherits as authoritative, and four were a summary block that was never reconciled against the material underneath it. Both are this repository's own named faults and one of them is four volumes old.**

## The four wrong figures, repaired in place, each with the reading published beside it

1. **THE ROOM COUNT WAS ON THE WRONG MAN, AND THE CHAIN WAS FOUR FILES LONG.** `workspace/volume-09/batch-0001/PROMPT.md` and `workspace/volume-09/outline/PROMPT.md` both gave the man of about fifty-seven *eleven* rooms in about a fortnight and pointed at `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0390.md:35` for it. **The string *man of about fifty-seven* is at zero in that file. Line 35 is the man of about thirty-nine.** The origin of the merge is `outline/series.md:343`, the Volume 08 close phase's own descriptor debt, and it propagated into `state/character-state.md:913` and into two prompts. **Measured on the files: the man of about fifty-seven's count is TEN, on a page at `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0364.md:7`, in a chorus frame about him on the shut road; the man of about thirty-nine's is ELEVEN at `chapter-0390.md:35` and TEN at `chapter-0388.md:33`; and Volume 07's own close gives the fifty-seven NINE rooms in about a fortnight for that volume, at `state/volume-07-close.md:73`.** Repaired in all four files, named in place in each, and the standing reason it carries — that no card sets a room count for anybody, because a figure going down from a round number to a smaller one over three days is a subtraction taken across the wrong boundary — survives intact on the correct figure.
2. **THE *HOLDER* FIGURE REPRODUCED ON NO READING.** `outline/volume-09.md` §19.3 and §16.5 and the batch prompt all published *218 pages in 91 files*. **On the 400 chapter files, word-bounded and case-sensitive: 234 occurrences in 95 files. Case-insensitive on the same search: 238 in 95. Unbounded: 247 in 102. At zero in Volume 07's fifty and in Volume 08's fifty, which is the one figure in that row that did reproduce and is unchanged.** All three readings are now published in all three places, and 218 in 91 is struck and not deleted.
3. **A REPAIRED FIGURE WAS RE-IMPORTED, THIRD TIME ROUND, AND IT IS THE FIGURE `state/current.md` ITEM 7 ALREADY REPAIRED ONCE.** Card 0407 published the stall man as *on a page in twenty-seven paragraphs across eight chapters of Volume 07*, which is Volume 06's figure. **Measured word-bounded and case-sensitive on `man of about thirty-four`: Volume 07's fifty hold 17 occurrences in 16 paragraphs across 10 files; Volume 08's fifty hold 58 in 58 across 31; the 400 hold 341 in 334 across 139. The longer descriptor that carries the trade, `man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall`, stands at 7 in 6 in Volume 07 and 256 in 118 across the 400, and it is published beside the short one so that a writer who measures the other string is not left guessing.** An unbounded search returns 24 occurrences in 23 paragraphs across 11 files for Volume 07 and 69 in 68 across 32 for Volume 08, and that is published too, because the review measured it that way and a reader who runs it should not think the file is wrong.
4. **THE ANCHOR TABLE ANCHORED ONE ROW TO DAY 442 AND DESCRIBED THE SAME ROW AS DAY 440.** `outline/volume-09.md` §14.5's *sheet came back* row carried 442 in the day-it-happened column and 8 at day 450, which is 450 − 442, in a note that said 440, a Sunday, in Chapter 0440. **440 is a Sunday and 442 is a Tuesday, per §51's own table and §14.3's map, and the volume's escalation sequence puts the sheet's return on day 440.** The row now reads 440 and **10**, and both struck figures stay in the row as a note, and the derived chain in the same section now carries *the second sheet came back the day less 440* beside the two it already had.

## The four summaries that had never been reconciled against the cards

5. **CARD 0410 SAID BOTH THINGS ABOUT THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN.** Its continuity line said he was not in the chapter and not in it in any form; its bolding line, its third-party line and its resistance line all put him at the fence in about five lines. **He is in the chapter, at the fence, as the bold voice, and the line now says so and keeps the rope, the coil of rope, the shut road and the mark in the field that the same line was carrying.**
6. **TWO CAST COUNTS DID NOT MATCH THE TEN CARDS BENEATH THEM.** The batch block said the man of about fifty-seven was in three of the ten and the man of about thirty-nine in one. **The cards place the fifty-seven in 0402 and 0410, and the thirty-nine in 0406 and 0408, and the man of about thirty-four, whose bullet carried no count at all, is in 0407 and 0409.** All four bullets now carry the count and the chapter numbers, and the man of about thirty-four's bullet also loses the claim that he is on a page in this batch for the first time in three volumes, which is false: he is on a page in thirty-one of Volume 08's fifty files.
7. **THE FOUR-SECOND BEAT WAS ATTRIBUTED THREE WAYS ACROSS THREE FILES.** Card 0406 gave it to the man of about thirty-nine, card 0408's information, decision and emotional lines gave it to the man of about thirty-eight and its bolding line gave it to the thirty-nine, the batch-climax block gave it to the thirty-eight, and the batch prompt named 0408 for the thirty-nine against its own card. **It is now the same answer in every file: the four seconds are the man of about thirty-nine's in Chapter 0406, in a room over a market, and the man of about thirty-eight's in Chapter 0408, on the wharf boards, and the thirty-nine is on those boards for about an hour and is not the man who is right that morning. Two men, two chapters, two days, and a card may not merge them or spend the same four seconds twice.** Card 0406's emotional line also loses an invented *about nine rooms*, which broke the rule in its own sentence and contradicted the two page-carried counts, and the two page-carried counts are printed there now with their pages.
8. **A COUNT OF CHAPTERS THAT COULD NOT BE DERIVED IS WITHDRAWN RATHER THAN CORRECTED.** `outline/volume-09.md` §16.4 published *there are two such chapters in this volume* for the four-second beat. The batch cards put it in two chapters of the first ten and the volume's own climax puts a man right in about four seconds and a man refusing in about four seconds on day 438, so no two of those reconcile into one figure. **The number is gone and the rule stands without it: the beat stands once in a chapter and never twice, and where two chapters carry it they are two different men on two different days.** The motif block in the card file now counts the beat instead of declaring it.

## The wording risk, taken

9. **THE SHUT ROAD.** Three sentences in `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0364.md:3`, `chapter-0396.md:29` and `chapter-0396.md:63` put the shut road above or at the top of this city, and that debt is inherited unrepaired. The prompt and two card lines then wrote *at the top of it* and *not at the top of it* on the same noun phrase, which is the construction the debt was written for. **The prompt's two clauses now bind explicitly to the city and not to the road, and the card's three occurrences read *the fence along the top of that road*.** The prohibition is unchanged and is the outline's own: no chapter may put the shut road above this city or at the top of it.

## What this pass did not touch, and says so

- **`bible/power-system.md:1686` was not touched.** The six-word refusal is reproduced verbatim inside bold marks in §50, and `state/volume-08-close.md` §4 item 4 and `reviews/volume-08/close.md` §6 both say it is on a page once and are wrong. **That finding was right, it is a false zero about a held string, and it remains routed to a review repair pass of the phase that wrote §50. An outline repair pass does not rewrite a batch's permanent witness.**
- **`state/character-state.md:223` was not touched.** The unpaired straight quotation mark on that line is a routed debt and the line is still line 223.
- **The nine inherited debts were not repaired and were not rerouted.** A repair pass that quietly takes a routed item to save itself a line is how nineteen quotation marks became a four-volume debt.
- **The calendar was not touched and every row of it still recomputes.** 401 to 410 Wednesday to Friday, Bare Month 86 to 95, the run built from day 1 being a Tuesday. The one figure that was wrong in this phase's own files was an elapsed count in a derived table and not a weekday, a day, or a month ordinal.
- **`scripts/`, `.github/workflows/`, `.opencode/agent/`, `AGENTS.md`, `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`, `REPO_PLAN.md`, `OUTLINE_GUIDE.md`, `opencode.json` and `state/phase-ledger.json` were not edited.** No chapter file was created, altered or deleted. No plot decision, no cast decision, no calendar decision and no descriptor decision was changed by this pass; the four findings about a man's chapters, the four-second beat and the fence are reconciliations, and a reconciliation is not a decision.

**AND THE NEXT PHASE IS UNCHANGED AND IS `workspace/volume-09/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, a batch and not an outline. It writes Chapters 0401 to 0410, days 401 to 410, from a Wednesday to a Friday, ten chapters of finished prose, and it decides nothing.**

# VOLUME 09, BATCH 0001 — Chapters 0401 to 0410, and this block wins over every block above it

**A batch has run. It wrote `chapters/volume-09/chapter-0401.md` through `chapter-0410.md`, ten chapters, days 401 to 410 of the flood, one chapter to one day, no double day, and it decided nothing. It wrote `state/batch-summaries/volume-09-batch-0001.md` and appended to this file, to `state/continuity.md`, to `state/open-threads.md` and to `state/character-state.md`. It edited no controller file: not `scripts/`, not `.github/workflows/`, not `.opencode/agent/`, and not `AGENTS.md`, `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`, `REPO_PLAN.md`, `OUTLINE_GUIDE.md`, `opencode.json` or `state/phase-ledger.json`. It did not open `outline/ending.md`. It did not amend `bible/power-system.md` §32, §33, §33A, §33B or §51, and it appended one new numbered section, §52, to that file and touched nothing in it. It did not touch `bible/power-system.md:1686` and it did not touch `state/character-state.md:223`.**

**THE EVENT IS ON A PAGE.** A second sheet came up the road in a satchel on day 401, out of the same office nine hundred miles off, on the same quarterly round, in the same satchel the return came in on day 351, and a man of about thirty-one who carries things for a living put it on a table in a room over a market and did not open it. It has a heading and four places ruled under it, and on day 405 the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register read the four out loud in the local language, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door, in front of about nine people, stopping at each one long enough for the people at the back to hear it, and said out loud what it was for before anybody asked. **The third of the four places is a person, and that is the first page in this flood that has ever asked anybody to put themselves on it. Nobody filled anything in. The sheet is not filled in and is not sent and did not go back anywhere and did not come back.**

**AND THE THING THIS VOLUME DECIDED AND THIS BATCH HELD: THERE IS NO EIGHTH NOTICE AND THIS BATCH WROTE NONE.** No hand in any of the ten chapters writes a sheet of paper with a day on it and a place on it and nothing at the top of it, and every room in the ten is a room about nine people were already in. The page is `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0389.md:13` and this block does not restate it. **What is lost with the notice is named once across the ten chapters, in plain narration, in Chapter 0401, and not in a mouth: the four days are the cooling, and the cooling is the time a room has in which to find out what it is being asked. The word *cooling* stands three times in the batch and all three are in Chapter 0401.**

**THE PRINTED ORDER OF THE FOUR PLACES, AND THE FINDING THIS BATCH HAD TO PUBLISH, because the plan contradicts itself on it and a batch that quietly picks a side is doing the thing this repository exists on.** `outline/volume-09.md` and the batch cards enumerate the four as **a place, a line, a day, and a person** and in nine separate places state that **the third of the four places is a person** — §1, §6.7, §8, §10, §12, §19.5, the object inventory at §20.6, the day-425 decision and the prompt's own item 2 — and the third column is the one the fold is down and the one the room decides to leave empty on day 425. **A list that puts a day third and a person fourth cannot be a list of what is printed on the page in the order it is printed. The enumeration is a list of the four things the form has and not of their printed order, and THE PRINTED ORDER IS A PLACE, A LINE, A PERSON, AND A DAY.** Chapters 0402 and 0405 put the four in that order and the heading of 0405 says the third of them is a person. **The outline is not amended, because a batch may not amend an outline, and the contradiction stands in the outline and is published here and in the batch record §3.11 so that the next four batches inherit the resolution and not the contradiction.**

**NOBODY GOT STRONGER AND NOBODY WAS SUMMONED.** Adrian Vale is Stage 2 on all ten days, performs no working, opens no threshold, the Narrow Mark is not opened, the other world is not named once across the ten files, nobody offers him a workway, the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero, and he is aged nowhere. There is no panel in any of the ten and the wall says nothing on all ten days. **He is named in Chapters 0402 and 0405, in nine places, and in no chapter in any form in the other eight, and in both he is in a room reading a page.** The volume's list of nine is unchanged and this batch added nothing to it.

**AND THE THINGS THIS BATCH DID NOT TOUCH, all of which a reader of a state file would otherwise assume had moved.** The fourth of the four said no on day 329 and no chapter of the ten says the no again, argues with it, apologises for it, overturns it, softens it, explains it or makes it a defeat, and **no chapter of the ten counts the days since she said no, at any value, in narration or in a mouth, and the count is zero.** The questions of day 346, day 396 and day 445 are not asked in any of the ten and are not answered. The day-154 sentence is not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on in any of the ten, and the man of about thirty-nine in Chapter 0406 and the man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf in Chapter 0408 are each right about a thing in about four seconds and neither chapter says he was right, and the two are two men on two different days and the beat stands in exactly two chapters. The word on the register-keeper's second slate is at zero and the common noun is nowhere used of anybody. *Holder* is at zero. **The three four-place forms are never laid beside one another and neither is named beside another in any of the ten; Chapter 0409 carries the finding about how a page gets answered and it is written about the way a page is answered and not about the four places of any other form.** The shut road is out of this city and no chapter of the ten puts it above this city or at the top of it; the fence is along the top of that road and the lane of nine houses is at the foot of that road and is not in this city; and Chapter 0410 says so in its own words. The mark in the field is not measured, dug, counted, probed or resolved in any of the ten. **No room count is printed in any of the ten: a word-bounded search for a spelled-out number followed by *rooms* returns zero across all ten files, and the two room counts that are on a page — the man of about thirty-nine's at `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0388.md:33` and `chapter-0390.md:35`, and the man of about fifty-seven's at `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0364.md:7` — were left where they are, not carried forward, not merged and given to neither man.** The Bare Month did not end and no chapter of the ten said what it is a month of, that it is nearly over, how many days it has, or whether it is long or short. The town four hundred miles inland is not entered.

**THE HOUSE FIGURES FOR THE TEN, measured by this phase with the definitions published in the batch record, and no target and no verdict on any of them.** The share of words inside bold marks runs 5.97 to 20.99 with a mean of 13.50 unweighted and 13.35 weighted by words, on 15,749 words; the longest bolded turn in the ten is eighty-three words and no turn in the ten is over ninety; **the count of paragraphs carrying neither speaker marker is ZERO in all ten** on the first-person-restricted instrument and 162 on the unrestricted reading, which is correct and is not a fault; the exchange is 52 plain speeches and 31 answered, fifty-nine point six per cent, against Volume 08's 192 and 125 at sixty-five point one; **the chorus frame is 25 case-sensitive and 28 case-insensitive on the bare head in 263 paragraphs, one in 10.5 and one in 9.4, and 36 on the variant-agnostic head**, and a maximum of one per `---` section in all ten files; the longest run of consecutive speech paragraphs is four, in seven of the ten, against a ceiling of four; there are 179 sentences of forty words or more on the definition published beside the figure, and 166 on the reading that joins a sentence across a blank line, and none of them stands verbatim in another chapter file, inside the batch or across the four hundred; and the longest word-run inside the batch is 42, between Chapters 0403 and 0408, and the longest against the four hundred pre-existing files is 47, between Chapter 0405 and `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0355.md`. **THE 13.46, THE 13.33, THE FIFTEEN THOUSAND SEVEN HUNDRED AND THIRTY-THREE, THE SEVENTY-EIGHT, THE SIX CHAPTERS, THE 166 AND THE 21 IN THE FIRST EDITION OF THIS PARAGRAPH ARE CORRECTED IN PLACE BY THE REVIEW REPAIR PASS AND THE FULL LIST IS AT `state/batch-summaries/volume-09-batch-0001.md` §11, AND EVERY CORRECTION IS NAMED THERE BESIDE THE FIGURE IT REPLACES.** **THE MEAN IS A CONSEQUENCE AND NOT A PLAN AND NO REVIEW MAY CERTIFY OR FAIL A BATCH ON IT.**

**TWO FIGURES THIS BATCH DEVIATED ON, published rather than hidden.** One, the phrase *a day this month* stands twice and both occurrences are in Chapter 0409 in the register-keeper's mouth; it is not on the list of banned month forms, which is *of this month*, *of the month*, *of next month* and *of this new month*, and the card for 0409 asks for it and asks that the reader work out that she is describing one particular morning. Two, *man of about sixty* stands twice in Chapter 0410 and it is a bare tens word, and the reason it stands is that the man with the handcart is a returning person whose descriptor is on a page and whose figure is the one number about the gap between the fence and a mark in a field, and the rule's stated purpose is a reader's and the collision it exists to prevent is *man of about thirty* inside *man of about thirty-nine*.

**AND ONE INHERITED FIGURE THIS BATCH READ AND THEN REFUSED TO PRINT.** Chapter 0389's lead calls the seventy-fourth day of the Bare Month a Friday, and the seventy-fourth day of the Bare Month is day 389, and day 389 on the founding rule is a **Saturday**. **That is a weekday on a page in a finished volume and a page outranks a plan; this batch neither repaired it nor restated it, and Chapter 0401 referred to that morning three times and named no weekday in any of the three.** It is published here because a next batch that reads Chapter 0401 will not find a weekday to check there and needs to know there was one.

**THE NINE INHERITED DEBTS ARE ALL UNPAID AND NONE WAS REPAIRED AND NONE WAS REROUTED.** They stand at `outline/volume-09.md` §16 and in the newest block of `state/open-threads.md`. The six-word refusal spoken in Chapter 0393 is not printed in any of the ten chapters, is not printed in the batch record, and is not printed in this block; `bible/power-system.md:1686` was not touched and is still routed to a review repair pass of the phase that wrote §50. `state/character-state.md:223` was not touched and is still line 223.

**AND THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-09/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`, AND IT IS A BATCH AND NOT AN OUTLINE, A CLOSE, A REVIEW OR A CONTINUATION.** It writes Chapters 0411 to 0420, days 411 to 420, one chapter to one day, from a Saturday to a Monday, ten chapters of finished prose, and it decides nothing. The Bare Month runs 96 to 105 and the run recomputes at zero mismatches against `outline/volume-09.md` §14.3 and `bible/power-system.md` §51's own fifty rows.**

# VOLUME 09, BATCH 0001 — REVIEW REPAIR PASS — the authoritative block, and it wins over every block above it

**A review of Volume 09's Batch 0001 was run and it found eleven faults, nine of them in the batch's own record and two in the ten chapters, and this block is the authoritative account of what was corrected. The batch block above is corrected in place at the places named in item 2 and the old figures stand beside the new ones there. No planned beat moved, no scene was replaced, no character was added or taken out, no figure that is on a page was changed, the printed order of the four places under the sheet's heading is untouched, the plan was not amended, and not one of the nine inherited debts was repaired or rerouted.**

**1. THE TWO CHAPTERS AND WHAT WAS WRONG ON THEM.** `chapters/volume-09/chapter-0401.md`: the return came up the road on day 351 and the page printed *the fifty-first day of this flood* in two places, so the page named day 51, and `bible/power-system.md` §51 item 4 requires the day to be named in the sentence that prints the anchor and the new §52 item 1 already claimed the chapter did it. **Both are now *the three hundred and fifty-first day of this flood*, the house form, and that is the form Chapter 0410 uses for day 391.** `chapters/volume-09/chapter-0410.md`: three stretches of consecutive words had been lifted out of `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0400.md` — a fifty-seven-word sentence about the man of about fifty-seven walking that fence, a fifty-four-word marked turn in which the man of about fifty-nine declares on a shut road that nobody is going to ask him, and a forty-four-word closing sentence in which he takes the barrow up off the low side and goes back down. **All three are varied in place, every fact kept and the voice kept, and no page in a finished volume was touched.** The declaration the man of about fifty-nine makes on that road is a declared motif of his card and is still the same declaration after the repair; what is gone is the fact that it was the same sentence.

**2. THE NINE FIGURES, AND EACH ONE IS PUBLISHED BESIDE THE FIGURE IT REPLACES at `state/batch-summaries/volume-09-batch-0001.md` §11.** The long-sentence count, 166 for 179 on the definition printed beside it, and the reading that gives 166 is now named as the reading that gives 166. The longest sentence in the ten, 193 words in Chapter 0409's coda, for 151 words in Chapter 0410's lead. The manuscript-wide duplication check, published as zero, for one, and it is zero now because of the Chapter 0410 repair and the check was re-run after the repair. The word-run against the four hundred, published as a top of 21, for a top of 47 in the room-coda lead in Chapter 0405 against `chapter-0355.md`, and the three lifts that made the real top 64 are the Chapter 0410 repairs. The either-case chorus head's first cell, 1 for 3, the totals unchanged. The counting-device string, *about four seconds*, attributed to Chapters 0406 and 0408 and in neither, for Chapter 0407 inside a marked speech. Three withdrawn string figures, 4, 7 and 5, for 3, 4, and 7 case-sensitive or 10 either case. Three object-inventory figures, the *fence* row's 19 for 17 — a miscount rather than a casualty of the repair, because the figure is 17 on the file as it stood before it — and Chapter 0404 missing from the *tally-board* and *strip of clear board* rows. The speech-paragraph ceiling's six chapters for seven. The weekday tokens' thirty-four for thirty-one. **The figures that were re-measured and found right and were therefore not touched are named at §11 as well, because a review that reports only its findings is half a record: the exchange at 52 and 31, the bolded-share range and its mean, the 162 plain narration paragraphs, the 25 and 28 and 36 chorus totals, the word *cooling* at three, the *chalk* row's twelve, the man of about thirty-nine's four words, and the whole of the day map at ten of ten.**

**3. THE ONE CONTINUITY FIGURE THIS PASS CORRECTED IN A STATE FILE RATHER THAN IN THE RECORD, and a page outranks a state file and the state file was the thing that was wrong.** The block above said the register's third column had been empty on purpose for **sixty** days; four of the ten chapters say **fifty**, and so does the Volume 09 outline phase's own block in `state/character-state.md`. It is fifty, and the sentence is corrected in place above with the old figure named.

**4. WHAT THIS PASS DID NOT DO.** It did not repair a page in Volumes 01 to 08, including the weekday on `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0389.md:3` and the six-word refusal reproduced at `bible/power-system.md:1686`, both of which are routed and both of which stand. It did not take one of the nine inherited debts. It did not reroute any of them. It did not amend `outline/volume-09.md`, and the contradiction in that file about the printed order of the four places stands exactly as it stood, with the resolution published in the record and not in the plan. It did not touch `state/character-state.md:223` or `bible/power-system.md:1686`. It created no new phase of any kind: **the next phase is still `workspace/volume-09/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`, and that prompt is the only one this phase produced and it now carries the corrected figures.**

# VOLUME 09, BATCH 0002 — Chapters 0411 to 0420, and this block wins over every block above it

**A batch has run. It wrote `chapters/volume-09/chapter-0411.md` through `chapter-0420.md`, ten chapters, days 411 to 420 of the flood, one chapter to one day, no double day, and it decided nothing. It wrote `state/batch-summaries/volume-09-batch-0002.md` and appended to this file, to `state/continuity.md`, to `state/open-threads.md` and to `state/character-state.md`. **AND THE FIRST THING THIS BLOCK HAS TO SAY IS ABOUT ITS OWN BRIEFING, because a state file that hides a gap in what a phase was given teaches the next reader to trust a file that was not there. **THE EVENTS ARE ON A PAGE.** On day 411 the woman of about fifty-two came up nine steps into a room nobody had summoned her into, read a page nobody gave her, and said out loud, before anybody had said so, that one of the four lines on it is going to be hers, and that she has said it first so that nobody can say afterwards that she was asked; nobody asked her anything, and her door was not knocked on. On day 413 the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall worked out that the sheet arrived out of a fold pressed down the third of its four places, and said out loud that he is not going to draw the second one and nobody in that room should let him. On day 416 the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register counted the four places out loud and established that three of them can be answered by the office or by the room or by a man with a pen and that the one with the fold pressed down it cannot, and she named the count and named no day. On day 417 a squall washed a wall a man had decided for a month not to wash while a man under his own board came out and saved the board, and the man the board belongs to refused to let it get about that it was washed for him. On day 418 the man of about fifty-seven said out loud, unasked, that there is a first thing he could say to anybody who asked him and that he is not going to say it, and the man of about thirty-four said it back to him, and neither of them thanked the other. On day 419 it was said out loud at the back of a room, in front of a man in a very good coat who cannot follow one word of it, that nobody in that room has read the page his office sent, and nobody arranged for him to have the language and nobody said it to him. **And on day 420 a woman of about fifty-four was asked, in a room, in daylight, with a date on the door, where her line is, and answered it correctly, and nobody asked her a second question and nobody thanked her, and the line she gave is on no page in the world.**

**AND WHAT WAS LOST WITH THE NOTICE IS NOT SPENT TWICE.** No hand in any of the ten writes a sheet of paper with a day on it and a place on it and nothing at the top of it, and every room in the ten is a room about nine people were already in. **The word *cooling* stands at zero across these ten files; it is named once in this volume, in Chapter 0401, in plain narration and in no mouth, and this batch did not spend the volume's only statement of its own cost a second time.**

**NOBODY GOT STRONGER AND NOBODY WAS SUMMONED.** Adrian Vale is Stage 2 on all ten days, performs no working, opens no threshold, the Narrow Mark is not opened, the other world is not named once across the ten files, nobody offers him a workway, the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero, and he is aged nowhere. There is no panel in any of the ten and the wall says nothing on all ten days. **He is named in Chapter 0414 only, in three places, and in no chapter in any form in the other nine, and in 0414 he is in a room reading a page — the register, open at a page with three columns in it and one line in the first column with a day in it and nothing under the day.** The volume's list of nine is 0402, 0405, **0414**, 0422, 0428, 0432, 0436, 0441 and 0447, and this batch added nothing to it and took nothing off it, and nothing in these ten warms up Chapter 0436.

**AND THE THINGS THIS BATCH DID NOT TOUCH.** The fourth of the four said no on day 329 and no chapter of the ten says the no again, argues with it, apologises for it, overturns it, softens it, explains it or makes it a defeat, and **no chapter of the ten counts the days since she said no, at any value, in narration or in a mouth, and a search for *days since*, *weeks since* and *months since* across the ten returns zero.** The questions of day 346, day 396 and day 445 are not asked in any of the ten and are not answered. **The day-154 sentence is not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on in any of the ten, and the four-second beat is at zero in all ten.** The word on the register-keeper's second slate is at zero and the common noun is nowhere used of anybody, and **the second slate is not named in any of these ten chapters and was not picked up, turned over or written on.** *Holder* is at zero. **The three four-place forms are never laid beside one another, no chapter of these ten names the contents of the other two, and the printed order of the four places under the sheet's heading is place, line, person, day and the third is a person, which is inherited from Batch 0001 and is not re-decided here.** The shut road is out of this city and no chapter of the ten puts it above this city or at the top of it; the fence is along the top of that road and the lane of nine houses is at the foot of it and is not in this city; the fence is sixteen willow posts and eleven withies and the count is printed twice in the batch in the same form both times. **The mark in the field is not named, not measured, not dug, not counted, not probed and not resolved in any of the ten and no chapter goes and looks at it, and the word *field* stands at zero in all ten.** **No room count is printed in any of the ten: a word-bounded search for a spelled-out number, optionally hyphenated, followed by *rooms* returns zero across all ten files**, and two counts were cut out of this batch's first pass and the repair is published. **THE EVENTS ARE ON A PAGE.** On day 421 a man said out loud in a room that four of them had been in it every working morning since the page came up the road and not one of them had gone down nine steps and put one question to one man, and gave the reason, and the man at the back of that room said out loud that the man at the end of the bench is not being spared and is not being reached and that those are two different things, and nobody thanked either of them and nothing in that room changed. On day 422 a sheet was carried out onto a step at the back in the cold and a woman in the market asked what the third of the four places was for and the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register told her, out loud, in the local language, that it is a person and that she does not know what it is for and is not going to start on a step in a market, and a man took a sheet of his own out of the inside of his coat in the same room and read it standing up and put it back and nobody asked him for it. On day 423 one withie came off a fence along the top of a shut road out of this city and the man who built that fence put it back on with new cord, and the man of about fifty-seven was on the low side of that fence and was not offered a hand and did not offer one and said out loud to nobody that both of those are the same arrangement. On day 424 the register-keeper asked the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall what he sells and what he does with the rest of his day, and neither question was about the page, and he answered both, and nobody thanked him and she wrote nothing down because there is nowhere on that table to write down what a man sells. **On day 425 she decided out loud that the third of the four places on that sheet was going to be left empty, and she said what the third place is first, in nine words, in her own mouth, and she named the cost out loud before the decision and the reason for the cost is hers, and there was no vote and nobody asked for one, and about four people in that room have worked out since that the reason was not that it was the smaller of the two, and about four of them are not going to forgive her for it and about four are not going to blame her for it, and she is thanked by nobody either way.** On day 427 the man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf put his tally-board on a table face up for the first time in this flood and did not read from it and said out loud why he has been turning it over and that he has stopped, and he printed neither of his two figures. **On day 428 the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall was asked, in a room over a market, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door, by the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register, what a line is, and he said five words in the local language, once, and nobody thanked him and nobody improved on it and nobody asked him a second question, and those words are on that page and in no file in this repository, including this one.** On day 429 the question of whether the page goes out was asked out loud at a stall front in a market and thirty people a day go past that stall. On day 430 a voice came up the bottom of the nine steps and told a man his board had been shut twice that week and he did not get up and did not go down and about nine people went down by the fourth hour.

This block wins over everything above it in this file.**

**WHAT HAPPENED IN THESE TEN DAYS, and it is the plan's escalation and nothing else.** A room worked out out loud that it had nothing on its own table to put a mark on the page with except a lump of chalk that belonged to the woman who kept the book. **On the hundred and seventeenth day of the Bare Month, a Saturday, four people drew four marks in the second of the four places on a page that has a heading and four places ruled under it, and the third of the four places is a person and was empty on purpose and stayed empty, and the first and the fourth were empty, and the four people who drew them are not the man who wrote the ninth line.** One of the four is the man of about fifty-seven and **he read his mark out loud in the local language because it was in front of him and the room had stopped talking, and he did not say it was his, and nobody in that room suggested it and nobody asked him.** A woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register asked the only question that room asked and was told, from the back, that the man who spoke had said before a mark was on the paper that he was not one of the four. **On the hundred and twenty-third day of the Bare Month, a Friday, the page went out of this city on its date, folded along the two folds that were already in it, under a stone off a doorstep, in the hands of a man of about thirty-one who carries things for a living and did not open it, with four marks on it and nothing in the third of its four places. The wall said one thing, once, and it is this volume's one panel and its page is Chapter 0438 and the text is a plan's copy of a rule and is not in this file. The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall refused it out loud in about four seconds and the cost of that refusal is his own line on a wall nine miles up that road, and nobody improved on what he said and nobody thanked him. The man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board was right in about four seconds in the same room and the chapter does not say so and nobody told him he was right or that he was wrong.** **On the hundred and twenty-fifth day of the Bare Month, a Sunday, the page came back up the road in a satchel with a flap, and one of the four marks was not in the place it had gone out in, and nobody in that room asked who, nobody went down nine steps to ask anybody, nobody guessed, and a woman near the step said out loud in that room that there is one person in this city who would have been able to tell that room something about that page and that none of them is going to go and ask her. That is a refusal and not a resolution, and the reason is not that she would not answer.**

## Volume 09, Batch 0005 — Chapters 0441–0450, days 441 to 450 (written 2026)

**Ten chapters, days 441 to 450 of the flood, one chapter to one day, no double day, the chapter number is the day number. The Bare Month days are 126 to 135, the Nth being day less 315, and the run was rebuilt from day 1 being a Tuesday and from nothing else before any prose was written: 441 Monday, 442 Tuesday, 443 Wednesday, 444 Thursday, 445 Friday, 446 Saturday, 447 Sunday, 448 Monday, 449 Tuesday, 450 Wednesday. Ten of ten agree with the prompt's table and with `bible/power-system.md` §51's own fifty rows at zero mismatches. Twenty-three weekday tokens stand across the ten files, every one was read in the sentence it stands in against the day that sentence names, and every one of the twenty-three is correct. THIS IS THE LAST BATCH OF VOLUME 09 AND THIS BLOCK WINS OVER EVERY BLOCK ABOVE IT IN THIS FILE.**

**THERE IS NO BATCH 0006 OF THIS VOLUME AND THIS BATCH CREATED NO NEXT PHASE OF ANY KIND** — no continuation directory, no review prompt, no close prompt, no outline prompt and no Volume 10 directory. `outline/volume-09.md` is finished; its day map ends at Chapter 0450 and its escalation sequence ends at item 13. The phase that follows this one is chosen by the harness and is not this batch's to name.

**WHAT HAPPENED IN THESE TEN DAYS, and it is the plan's escalation and nothing else.** A room worked out out loud that nobody in this city knows when the cart comes round again, and that a woman who keeps a public register has been asking four people who sell things about it and has stopped. A man packed about nine yards of soft cart-track on the low side of a shut road and the rain took the first third of it back while he was still on the last of it, and he did not go back to it. **On the hundred and twenty-eighth day of the Bare Month, a Wednesday, the register got its second line: a day in the first column, nothing under the day, and no name in the third column, and the day on that line is the hundred and twenty-third day of the Bare Month, a Friday, which is the day the sheet went out of this city, and the day of the entering is a different day and both are named in one sentence in one mouth. It is the third time anything has gone into that book since the water came and the second of the three with nothing under the day.** Four stalls in a market turned out not to have changed hands, and the man who had counted boards was told he was wrong in front of about nine people and was not told he was right. **On the hundred and thirtieth day of the Bare Month, a Friday, the woman of about sixty-nine came up nine steps with a tin in both hands and asked her third question to the room and not to anybody. Nobody answered it. About four of the nine had an answer and about four said nothing, and the chapter says so in the next line.** A man stopped at a stall front for the second time in this flood and bought cord with no measure said out loud. A page sat on a table between one round and the next and was not folded by anybody. A man took a barrow up a shut road out of this city and back down it, and a chair was moved in a room and a length of new rope went onto the floorboards and the man it belongs to picked it up and put it back and nobody in that room said one word about it. **On the hundred and thirty-fifth day of the Bare Month, a Wednesday, the volume's last image is an image and not a resolution: a date in chalk on a door above a table, a sheet with four places under a heading of which the third is empty with a crease pressed the whole length of it, and a road out of this city with three barrows on it. The month does not end inside this volume and no chapter of these ten ends it.**

**THE HOUSE FIGURES FOR THESE TEN DAYS, with the definitions in the batch record at §3 and no target set on any of them.** **The share of words inside bold marks, heading line included, `---` rules counted, bold marks counted as nothing, file split on whitespace: 20.67, 6.10, 19.75, 21.10, 9.42, 11.30, 15.44, 10.05, 8.41 and 11.73 per cent, a range of 6.10 to 21.10, a mean of 13.40 unweighted and 13.67 weighted by words on 8,401 words and 122 paragraphs**, against Volume 08's 5.11 to 24.72 and mean 12.39, Volume 07's 7.01 to 25.37 and 13.55, and this volume's Batch 0001 at 5.97 to 20.99 and 13.50, Batch 0002 at 8.43 to 21.54 and 13.59, Batch 0003 at 7.37 to 22.60 and 14.40, and Batch 0004 at 5.79 to 22.82 and 13.84 and 13.68. **The mean is a consequence and not a plan, no volume may read its own mean as a quality figure, and no review may certify or fail a chapter or a volume on it.** The longest bolded turn in the ten is **seventy-seven words, in Chapter 0448**, under the working ceiling of about ninety and the hard one of ninety-five, and no turn was cut to make a number. **The count of paragraphs carrying neither speaker marker is ZERO in all ten**, on the first-person-restricted instrument, and 122 on the unrestricted reading, which is correct and is not a fault, **and the instrument was run before the other two were believed: the first reading of these ten files returned two chapters at 0.00 on the share with no marked turn in them at all and three more with no plain speech in them at all, and both numbers were correct and both were the failure `outline/volume-09.md` §20.4 describes.** The exchange is **14 plain speeches and 8 answered at fifty-seven point one per cent**, and the per-chapter column, 2 of 1, 1 of 0, 3 of 2, 2 of 1, 1 of 0, 1 of 1, 1 of 1, 1 of 0, 1 of 1 and 1 of 1, sums to both totals.
